Chou and Wu give the fate of an official among monks or Daoists. 〈Recorded cases—**Guisi / Jiwei:** Zhu Xizhou, palace-examination first and minister. **Dingchou / Dingwei:** Pan Sheng, palace-examination second and minister; entered the Grand Secretariat and declined the appointment. **Jiwei / Yihai:** a minister. **Dinghai / Jichou:** a minister of Revenue. **Yichou / Jichou:** an assistant surveillance commissioner. **Yimao / Jichou:** a provincial graduate. **Wuzi / Bingchen:** a ministry secretary. **Renwu / Gengxu:** a supervising secretary. **Wushen / Renxu:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jisi / Jiaxu:** a metropolitan graduate. **Xinhai / Dingyou:** Zou Yuanbiao, minister of Personnel. **Jiashen / Bingyin:** Hanlin Reviewer Chu Taichu; wealth invited calamity, and he was executed in the marketplace. **Gengxu / Bingxu:** “Jia ministry,” designation uncertain. **Renzi / Xinhai:** a court minister. **Gengshen / Wuzi:** Vice Minister Wang Zuo.〉

**Renshen day.** Distinction. Chen, Xu, Chou, and Wei give auspicious Mixed-Qi Wealth and Officer. No Ji Earth in the year or month stems permits entry into the Day-Lu configuration. You month and Shen or Mao year give manifest distinction in northeast luck. 〈Recorded cases—**Renshen / Bingwu:** Minister Liu Anfeng. **Yimao / Xinsi:** a censor. **Yichou / Guiwei:** a censor. **Bingzi / Dingyou:** Chen Kai, left censor-in-chief and head of the Censorate, from Jiangxi; an ordinary man from Nanjing shared the chart. **Jiazi / Yihai:** a censor. **Jisi / Jiaxu:** a supervising secretary. **Gengzi / Renwu:** a metropolitan graduate. **Gengchen / Jimao:** a ministry secretary. **Xinyou / Dingchou:** a vice minister. **Xinwei / Renchen:** a provincial graduate. **Gengxu / Jichou:** a provincial graduate. **Dingwei / Gengxu:** Yang Qiyuan, Hanlin scholar and vice minister.〉[^138]

**Renxu day.** Wealth and distinction in youth. Hai, Shen, Si, and Xu in year and month give first- or second-rank distinction; pure Xu with Water-and-Fire luck gives gold and purple. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiawu / Bingyin:** Vice Minister Hu Ding. **Guihai / Yichou:** Vice Minister Huang Yangmeng. **Renzi / Jiyou:** minister of the Court of Judicial Review. **Xinyou / Xinchou:** a provincial administration commissioner. **Bingshen / Wuxu:** Chief Censor Zhou Yong. **Renyin / Xinhai:** Chief Censor Han Yong, an eminent minister. **Jiashen / Bingzi:** a deputy commissioner. **Bingyin / Xinchou:** a senior provincial administrator. **Jiayin / Yihai:** a bureau director. **Gengwu / Renwu:** a censor. **Dingsi / Bingwu:** a provincial graduate. **Jimao / Dingmao:** a deputy commander. **Dingwei / Renchen:** Grand Secretary Wei Yunzhong.〉

〈Lu and Horse in Mutual Passage:  
When the time arrives, name and profit are complete.  
Without punishment, clash, control, or breaking,  
One can rise to immortality from level ground.〉

> Ren and Xin meeting are judged at Hai hour;  
> Do not resent white jade for coming to light late.  
> Birth, Lu, and Horse without punishment or breaking:  
> Cast off hempen clothes and don the purple robe.

> A Ren day approaches Lu and Horse;  
> It is also Seal-Ribbon in One Country.  
> Water follows Metal and Wood, naturally strong:  
> This fate is supremely high, of the upper order.  
> Gui and Yi combining secretly [diminish] blessing;  
> Without clash or breaking, literary brilliance appears.  
> Jade accumulated, gold piled high, fill the hall:  
> An image of hereditary favor for sons and a title for the wife.[^139]

## 49. Gui Days, Renzi Hour
### 六癸日子時斷
<!-- source: L2268-L2313 -->

> A Gui day born at Renzi hour:  
> A road through the azure clouds is supreme excellence.  
> Without Ji Earth to clash, control, and break,  
> Achievement will have its time of manifest advancement.

On a Gui day at Renzi hour, the Day’s Lu Returns to the Hour. Gui Water establishes Lu upon Zi. If year and month’s stems and branches contain no Wu, Ji, Wu, or Wei bringing punishment, clash, breaking, or harm, and the Three Origins have support and communicate with the month’s qi, literary talent is refined and office distinguished. A month communicating with Wood qi also gives distinction. Ji exposed in the pillars, with Jia to combine with it, likewise gives distinction; otherwise there are reversals.[^140]

**Guichou day.** Wu, Yin, Yichou, and Jichou in year and month give fourth or fifth rank in civil office. A body-strengthening Wood-qi month, without Wu, Ji, Wu, or Si, gives manifest distinction. Wei gives censorial office; Si and Wu give metropolitan success. Avoid Renyin, Yiwei, and Gengxu months, all bringing punishment; Yichou month gives low station. 〈Recorded cases—**Renxu / Wushen:** a prefect. **Renzi / Guichou:** a censor. **Gengchen / Xinsi:** a ministry secretary. **Guisi / Gengshen:** a metropolitan graduate. **Xinmao / Xinchou:** a metropolitan graduate.〉

**Guimao day.** Hour and day punish each other, injuring wife and children. Nearness to the noble; without Ji Earth there is imposing authority. Hai and Si both complete give metropolitan success and office in the Censorate. Pure Yin in year and month gives Hurting Officer Generating Wealth: either great distinction or great riches. Avoid Jiachen month, ominous punishment and solitude; Bingshen month, an evil death; Jichou month, punishment. 〈Recorded cases—**Renyin / Renyin:** the highest rank; a rich man shared the chart. **Renyin / Geng–[branch uncertain]:** Minister Dai Yao. **Renwu / Guichou:** a provincial graduate. **Yiyou / Wuyin:** a supervising secretary in Nanjing. **Guihai / Guihai:** poverty. **Wuwu / Xinyou:** misfortune.〉[^141]

**Guisi day.** Spring and summer develop Wealth and blessing. Chen, Xu, Chou, and Wei give eventual riches if not distinction. Avoid Wuyin month, encountering punishment; Wushen month, suffering punishment; Jiyou month, beggary. 〈Recorded cases—**Bingyin / Bingshen:** a censor. **Gengyin / Yiyou:** a deputy commissioner. **Wushen / Gengshen:** Honorary Official Tang, whose son Binyin was metropolitan-examination first and palace second. The following flattened record gives **Guichou / Yichou**, with two captions: Chu Guoxian of Wujin, successful in the Guiyou and Bingxu examinations, a ministry secretary; and Lu Shi of Yuyao, successful in Xinmao and Jiachen, a ministry secretary. **Jiwei / Dingchou:** a deputy surveillance commissioner.〉[^142]

**Guiwei day.** Shen and You months give distinction; Xu and Hai great wealth and distinction; Yin and Mao solitude; Chen and Si misfortune. Avoid Renshen month, bodily mutilation and misfortune; Gengchen month, ominous punishment; Yichou month, loss of land and violent death. 〈Recorded cases—**Guihai / Renxu:** Xu Jie, palace-examination third and grand secretary. **Yiyou / Wuzi:** a prefect. **Guiwei / Jiazi:** a pacification commissioner. **Jiawu / Dingchou:** a court usher.〉

**Guiyou day.** Engagement and withdrawal, advancement and retreat; few sons, and difficulty with the wife’s family. Avoid Bingyin month, death with the body mutilated; Yisi month, great defeat and violent death; Dingyou month, death by hanging. 〈Recorded cases—**Guiyou / Wuwu:** Xu Zhi, minister of the Court of Judicial Review. **Yiyou / Bingxu:** a censor. **Xinwei / Gengzi:** a provincial graduate. **Xinyou / Gengchen:** third rank. **Gengchen / Xinsi:** a provincial graduate. **Jisi / Jiaxu:** a remonstrance official. **Dingyou / Renyin:** Minister Xu Yuantai. **Renwu / Wushen:** an associate subprefect of Gaoyou, executed in the marketplace over river works. **Jiazi / Yihai:** the Ming Shenzong emperor’s empress, with Heavenly and Monthly Virtue complete.〉

**Guihai day.** Distinction. Hai-month birth gives Flying Heavenly Lu and Horse, high examination success, and great distinction. Gui’s Lu dwells at Zi and Ren’s at Hai: day and hour exchange them, giving third- or fourth-rank distinction. Avoid Wuyin month, death with the body mutilated; Wushen month, solitude and poverty; Jiyou month, solitude and hardship. 〈Recorded cases—**Guihai / Jiazi:** Old Peng; the body returns to winter flourishing, hence longevity. **Dingsi / Guimao:** Grand Secretary Zhang Zhen. **Yiyou / Gengchen:** Chief Censor Song Rike. **Jiaxu / Dingchou:** a prefect. **Yichou / Wuzi:** a provincial graduate. **Guisi / Guihai:** a tribute scholar; died.〉

〈Obtaining Lu and Generating Wealth:  
Withdrawn in depth, passage opens elsewhere.  
When the time arrives, name and profit follow smoothly;  
Wealth and distinction display the family’s standing.〉

> The Day’s Lu Returns to the Hour within the configuration;  
> Food God is welcome; punishment and clash are feared.  
> Do not say Hurting Officer injures Wealth luck;  
> Without Officer added, Wealth is not abundant.[^143]

> A Gui day approaching Renzi hour:  
> It shares the name of the Returning-Lu configuration.  
> Even an unadorned household rises impressively;  
> The Dark Warrior holds authority, and Lu is weighty.  
> Water clear, the Treasure Vase grows fuller;  
> Literary learning ranges widely and penetrates deeply.  
> Honored advancement comes through Purple Tenuity’s investiture;  
> Jia, Wu, Yin, and Hai bring breaking and disturbance.[^144]

## 50. Gui Days, Guichou Hour
### 六癸日丑時斷
<!-- source: L2314-L2365 -->
〈The unfavorable months stated for the six Gui days below are judged as above, together with the relevant Gui-day hour.〉

> A Gui day born at Guichou hour:  
> A hidden ghost in the branch brings punishment and injury.  
> A month communicating with bodily strength requires care for the wife;  
> Chou remotely combining with Si gives extraordinary distinction.

On a Gui day at Guichou hour, the branch contains a hidden ghost. Gui takes Ji as Indirect Officer; hidden Ji within Chou has its proper position. Gui takes Ding as wife, but Ding Fire has no qi within Chou. A body-strengthening Companions month raises concern for loss of wife and Wealth. Many Chou and Yin in the pillars let Yin punish Si and Chou combine with Si, drawing forth Bing and [Wu] within Si as Wealth and Officer through punishment and combination. With neither Wu nor Ji in the exposed stems, great distinction follows. Si, Wei, and Mao are avoided as breaking the configuration.[^145]

**Guichou day.** Chou remotely invites Bing and [Wu] within Si as Wealth and Officer. Chou, Yin, Shen, Zi, and You, with Triple Combination in year and month, give a worthy wife, filial children, and exceptional honor and distinction. Fire-and-Earth luck is ominous; Metal-and-Water luck auspicious. 〈One account says:〉 solitary, losing land, yet noble. 〈Recorded cases—**Guisi / Renxu:** Minister Zhang Zan. **Xinchou / Xinchou:** Vice Minister Sun En. **Bingshen / Xinchou:** Grand Secretary Wang Jiaping, from Shanyin in Shanxi; an impoverished scholar whose place of origin is missing shared the chart. **Jiawu / Bingyin:** a ministry secretary. **Renwu / Renyin:** Chief Censor Song Chan. **Xinwei / Jihai:** a senior provincial administrator. **Guichou / Guihai:** a minister. **Bingxu / Xinmao:** a supervising secretary. **Guiyou / Guihai:** a bureau director. **Wuyin / Wuwu:** a commander-in-chief. **Renxu / Guichou:** a vice censor-in-chief. **Gengshen / Gengchen:** a senior provincial administrator. **Gengshen / Yiyou:** Yu Shilian, minister of the Nanjing Court of Imperial Horses.〉[^146]

**Guimao day.** Day Nobility: solitude and injury, yet distinction. Chou, Yin, and Chen in year and month, with neither Wu, Ji, Si, Wu, nor Mao among the stems and branches, give the highest rank. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingchou / Jiachen:** Minister Weng Dali, from Zhejiang; a licentiate from the Central Region shared the chart. **Jiayin / Bingyin:** great distinction. **Bingzi / Gengzi:** a school instructor. **Renchen / Jiyou:** an earl. **Guimao / Wuwu:** an earl. **Jichou / Dingchou:** a man subjected to punishment. **Yimao / Renwu:** a senior provincial administrator.〉

**Guisi day.** The Blessing, Virtue, and Refined-Qi configuration: learning, intelligence, outstanding talent, exceptional advancement, and distinction. Pure Chou and Si in year and month give great distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuxu / Jiazi:** Minister Wang Ji of Dengzhou. **Wuzi / Jiayin:** a subprefect; a military student shared the chart. **Wuwu / Guihai:** distinction develops.〉

**Guiwei day.** Zi, Chou, and Yin in year and month give distinction, with wealth in middle life. Mao month with Metal-and-Water luck gives the highest rank; Chen and Xu complete the Four Storehouses and give great distinction. 〈One account says:〉 humble and lowly at first, distinguished only in middle life, with injury to parents. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiaxu / Xinwei:** a Grand Tutor. **Yichou / Wuzi:** a provincial administration commissioner. **Renxu / Gengxu:** a metropolitan graduate. **Wuyin / Bingchen:** a provincial graduate. **Guihai / Renxu:** seven sons. **Dingwei / Renyin:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jiyou / Bingyin:** “Jia ministry,” designation uncertain; without sons, died in Wuxu. **Gengshen / Jiashen:** a metropolitan graduate.〉

**Guiyou day.** The Blessing, Virtue, and Refined-Qi configuration: profound learning, advances and retreats in public engagement, and difficulty for the wife’s household. 〈One account says:〉 imprisonment, poverty, and low station. Zi and Si in year and month with Geng and Xin exposed mean the month conceals the Three Wonders and the year obtains Lu and Seal: first-rank distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Guichou / Xinyou:** Chief Censor Fu Shiyuan. **Jiazi / Dingchou:** a supervising secretary for Personnel. **Guimao / Yimao:** an official in the Bureau of Carriages. **Yiwei / Jiashen:** Grand Secretary Zhu Geng. **Jisi / Renshen:** Grand Secretary Qian Xiangkun. **Bingzi / Guisi:** “Jia ministry,” designation uncertain. **Bingshen / Bingshen:** a senior provincial administrator.〉

**Guihai day.** Arching Lu, which fears clash, breaking, and filling the vacant position. Without Zi, Si, Wu, or Wei in the pillars, there is great distinction. Yin, Wu, and Xu months give sixth or seventh rank in southern luck. 〈Recorded cases—**Guimao / Yichou:** Ke Qian, palace-examination first. **Gengyin / Wuzi:** Yu Shen, palace-examination first. **Dingchou / Guichou:** a privy councillor. **Dingwei / Guimao:** a chancellor. **Xinwei / Wuxu:** a senior provincial administrator. **Yichou / Jichou:** a censor. **Yichou / Jiashen:** a chief censor. **Jihai / Gengwu:** Hanlin Reviewer Cheng Xian. **Jimao / Bingyin:** a ministry secretary. **Gengwu / Renwu:** a metropolitan graduate. **Yisi / Wuyin:** an empress dowager. **Dingyou / Yisi:** a metropolitan graduate.〉

〈A Golden Coffer Stored within the Storehouse:  
Wei appears—the key opens it.  
Without a key, it is locked in vain;  
Later prospects answer the heart’s desires.〉

> Repeated yin Water is received by the hour’s Storehouse;  
> Youth finds advancement hard—do not force the quest.  
> Once the trials of middle life have been endured,  
> Sit at ease in the high hall and let the hair grow white.

> A Gui day meeting Guichou hour:  
> Flowing Water fills and surges through the Metal configuration.  
> The Storehouse meets Xu and Wei: Lu and Wealth abound.  
> Not passing beyond emptiness and want, it is hard to move.  
> No key, and youth remains undistinguished;  
> A key present, Lu and Horse are in harmony.  
> When luck arrives, why torment the heart with toil?  
> Advancement brings great rejoicing to the family gate.[^147]

## 51. Gui Days, Jiayin Hour
### 六癸日寅時斷
<!-- source: L2366-L2417 -->

> A Gui day born at Jiayin hour:  
> Blade returns upon Officer and Lu, diminishing the spirit.  
> With neither Geng nor Shen in the pillars,  
> Punishment Combining Wealth and Officer makes a noble person.[^148]

On a Gui day at Jiayin hour, punishment combines Wealth and Officer. Gui takes Bing as Wealth and Wu as Officer. Yin punishes forth Bing and Wu within Si as Wealth and Officer. If Officer and Killings are absent from the pillars, and punishment, clash, breaking, and harm do not damage the configuration, there is distinction. Geng, Shen, Wu, or Ji without restraint prevent distinction. 〈A gap in the original.〉

**Guichou day.** Noble in principle, but Xin Metal within Chou reduces its measure. Pure Water in year and month brings office as high as first rank; autumn birth gives Seal-Ribbon and also distinction. Chen month with eastern luck gives poverty and low station; pure Shen breaks Yin and is ominous. 〈Recorded cases—**Renshen / Guichou:** Grand Secretary Gao Gong, without sons, from the Central Region; a Zhejiang licentiate shared the chart. **Yisi / Jichou:** great distinction. **Renwu / Wushen:** Vice Minister Shi Maohua. **Bingzi / Xinmao:** a county magistrate. **Gengchen / Renwu:** a provincial graduate. **Jiwei / Jiaxu:** a chief surveillance commissioner. **Renwu / Renyin:** Song Xun, minister of Personnel.〉

**Guimao day.** Hai and Mao Wood months give the highest distinction. Yin and Mao in year and month give the Punishment-Combination configuration and a marquis or earl; Yin and Hai give fourth rank. Yin month with western luck gives gold and purple; Chou, Xu, Chen, and Si are also distinguished. 〈Recorded cases—**Guiyou / Jiwei:** Cai Qing, chancellor of the Directorate of Education. **Gengxu / Guiyou:** an academician. **Yihai / Bingxu:** a military commander. **Jiwei / Dingchou:** Chief Censor Zeng Xian; violent death. **Yihai / Wuyin:** a deputy commissioner. **Xinmao / Gengzi:** a commander-in-chief. **Guichou / Jiayin:** a senior provincial administrator. **Guiyou / Xinyou:** a senior provincial administrator. **Guichou / Xinyou:** a salt-transport commissioner. **Yihai / Wuzi:** a metropolitan graduate. **Gengshen / Wuzi:** distinction.〉

**Guisi day.** Ordinary. A month communicating with bodily strength gives great distinction. Wu, Ji, Geng, and Shen are avoided; years and luck follow the same rule. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiazi / Renshen:** Minister Wu Yue. **Yimao / Bingxu:** Vice Minister Yang Yi. **Wuyin / Wuwu:** Vice Minister Du Zheng. **Guihai / Jiazi:** a supervising secretary. **Gengchen / Dinghai:** a censor. **Gengchen / Yiyou:** a provincial graduate. **Guiyou / Jiazi:** “Jia ministry,” designation uncertain. **Guichou / Yichou:** a man of Shangyuan, poor, blind, and short-lived. **Jiachen / Yihai:** a provincial circuit official who repeatedly met calamity.〉[^149]

**Guiwei day.** Refinement and substance, with manifest distinction in middle life. Jiwei or Jisi in year and month gives military distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiazi / Guiyou:** a surveillance commissioner. **Guiwei / Dingsi:** wealth. **Renwu / Wushen:** an official in Personnel. **Renwu / Jiachen:** a metropolitan graduate. **Xinhai / Jiawu:** a provincial administration commissioner.〉

**Guiyou day.** The Metal-Spirit configuration. Yin, Wu, and Xu months complete Fire’s configuration, giving exceptional advancement and office of second or third rank. Chou year is avoided and gives no distinction. Si, Wei, Hai, and Zi in year and month give wealth and longevity; Chen or Chou, with Jia and Ding exposed in the stems, are ominous. 〈One account says:〉 an eminent worthy serving as a bridge and crossing. 〈Recorded cases—**Xinhai / Dingyou:** a minister. **Jiaxu / Jiaxu:** a vice minister. **Yiwei / Jiashen:** “Fuhan,” caption uncertain. **Wuxu / Jiayin:** a senior provincial administrator. **Guihai / Jiazi:** a provincial examination leader. **Jiazi / Jiaxu:** a county magistrate. **Jichou / Jisi:** Hanlin probationer Shen Wei, drowned in Renshen year. **Wushen / Gengshen:** a deputy court minister. **Jisi / Wuchen:** first in the provincial examination.〉[^150]

**Guihai day.** Zi, Chou, Wei, and Shen months give metropolitan success and censorial office in Metal-and-Water luck. Mao and Xu in year and month form a Sixfold Combination and give distinction. 〈Another account says:〉 violent death amid distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Renzi / Guichou:** an academician. **Yiwei / Guiwei:** a military governor. **Jiachen / Dingmao:** a censor. **Xinwei / Gengzi:** a county magistrate. **Bingshen / Yiwei:** first in the provincial examination. **Bingxu / Bingshen:** a metropolitan graduate. **Guisi / Renxu:** distinction. **Dinghai / Guihai:** distinction. **Bingzi / Guisi:** a palace eunuch.〉

〈The Sun Emerging through Mist and Rosy Cloud:  
At the limit of Obstruction, Peace arrives with passage.  
The common person gains substantial blessing;  
The gentleman advances along his path.〉

> Jiayin on a Gui day opens Wu and Bing;  
> Youth, not yet favored, remains concealed.  
> If the Four Pillars have neither clash nor breaking,  
> One climbs the clouds from level ground to the provincial terrace.

> A Gui day at Yin hour brings its fulfillment;  
> Combining stems and branches shine with honor.  
> Without Ren, Si, Wu, Geng, or Shen,  
> Wealth and Lu must grow abundant and rich.  
> Luck reaches the imperial city, and advancement follows;  
> Literary talent wins a name upon the tiger list.  
> But let one character meet even slight Void or clash:  
> Children harmed, wife injured, and stipend stripped away.[^151]

## 52. Gui Days, Yimao Hour
### 六癸日夘時斷
<!-- source: L2418-L2463 -->

> A Gui day born at Yimao hour:  
> Upon Birth’s ground, it encounters Food God.  
> Without Wu and You, and Xin and Ji besides,  
> Blessing and longevity are complete, with office and Lu.

On a Gui day at Yimao hour, Food God flourishes in the stem. Gui takes Yi as the Hall of Learning and Food God. Gui Water is at Birth upon Mao, and Yi sits upon Lu. If the pillars have no Ji to break, Xin to seize, or Wu and You to punish and clash, communication with the month’s qi and support give intelligence, longevity, office, and emolument. Ji Earth present prevents distinction. Spring-month birth gives advancement in northern luck.[^152]

**Guichou day.** Chen and Chou months give high distinction. 〈One account says:〉 low station in youth, distinction in middle life. 〈Recorded cases—**Yichou / Jichou:** a vice minister. **Jiayin / Dingmao:** a provincial graduate. **Renyin / Wushen:** Junior Provincial Administrator Long Zongwu, banished to frontier service. **Jiachen / Bingyin:** a county magistrate.〉

**Guimao day.** Hardship. Yin- or Mao-month birth gives the Hurting-Officer configuration; wealth and distinction are difficult to affirm. Wei and Xu in year and month give artistic skill and proximity to the noble. Chen and Chou are auspicious; Jichou and Bingzi ominous. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuwu / Gengshen:** Academician Lin Zhi. **Guimao / Dingsi:** vice chancellor of the Directorate of Education. **Gengwu / Jichou:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jiayin / Xinwei:** a salt-transport commissioner. **Gengchen / Gengchen:** a provincial graduate. **Dingmao / Jiachen:** a provincial graduate. **Bingshen / Xinchou:** a first-rank titled lady.〉

**Guisi day.** Wealth and Officer in Double Beauty. Spring gives Hurting Officer; summer flourishing Wealth; autumn stable Seal; winter an ordinary fate. Chou, Wu, Zi, and Hai in year and month bring third or fourth rank. Yihai and Yiyou give disability. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingchou / Jiyou:** an expositor-in-waiting. **Jiyou / Gengwu:** Minister Zhang Jiashan, from Jimo. **Gengxu / Renwu:** Senior Provincial Administrator Ding Cilü, banished to frontier service. **Bingchen / Jihai:** a court minister.〉[^153]

**Guiwei day.** Yin, Hai, Mao, and Wei months give Hurting Officer wholly removing Officer: firmness and decisiveness, but an ordinary fate. Chen, Xu, Shen, and Zi months give distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiaxu / Renshen:** a grand secretary. **Renchen / Xinhai:** an education commissioner. **Jihai / Wuchen:** a censor. **Jiyou / Jiaxu:** a metropolitan graduate. **Gengshen / Wuzi:** a metropolitan graduate. **Yichou / Jichou:** Zhao Shuaiyin, palace-examination third.〉

**Guiyou day.** Shen, Zi, and Chen months give distinction; Yin, Wu, and Xu a middling fate; Hai, Mao, and Wei an ordinary one; Si, You, and Chou wealth. 〈Recorded cases—**Jimao / Wuchen:** a prefect. **Guihai / Jiashen:** Minister Guo Yingpin.〉

**Guihai day.** Chen and Si months give censorial office. 〈Recorded cases—**Gengyin / Jimao:** a senior provincial administrator. **Jihai / Dingsi:** an education commissioner. **Yiwei / Gengchen:** a metropolitan graduate. **Gengzi / Guiwei:** a censor. **Jichou / Bingzi:** Cao Xun, metropolitan-examination first and palace third. **Dingwei / Gengxu:** a marquis.〉

〈Literary Star Enjoying Lu:  
The name is heard wherever one goes.  
Ren and Geng flourishing:  
The gentleman rises to minister or duke.〉

> Yi and Gui meet upon Food’s flourishing branch;  
> Heaven’s work in creating things is without partiality.  
> As luck proceeds, an eminent person recommends;  
> A hand plucks scarlet cassia on the road through clouds.

> A Gui day meeting Yimao hour:  
> A country where the noble enjoy Lu.  
> Yimao in the Jade Hall gives the place of marquis or prince,  
> Or minister and general at the Golden Gate.  
> The gentleman’s literary brilliance spreads abroad;  
> The common person’s Wealth and Lu fill the chests.  
> Jiayin and Xinyou bring considerable settled ease:  
> Riches, distinction, and splendor are greatly enjoyed.[^154]

## 53. Gui Days, Bingchen Hour
### 六癸日辰時斷
<!-- source: L2464-L2515 -->

> A Gui day born at Bingchen hour:  
> Indirect Officer without qi does not yet mean poverty.  
> If no Wood qi communicates with the configuration,  
> Surely a person of pure distinction, blessing, and Lu.

On a Gui day at Bingchen hour, the body sits upon the Officer Storehouse. Gui uses Wu and Ji as Officer; Earth’s Tomb at Chen is the Officer Storehouse. Bing is Wealth, but Chen forms a Water configuration, leaving Bing Fire without qi while Gui Water combines into the configuration. No Jia in the pillars to break Officer and damage the Storehouse gives distinction.

**Guichou day.** Ordinary. Chen, Xu, Chou, and Wei months give Wealth and Officer qi and bring distinction. 〈Another account says:〉 wealth and solitude. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingchou / Xinchou:** a provincial graduate. **Yisi / Wuyin:** a deputy court minister. **Bingyin / Renchen:** an imperial son-in-law.〉

**Guimao day.** Solitary, with hardship for father and mother. Wealth is present, and noble people show esteem. Mao and Hai in year and month give Day Nobility; Wu and Xu give rank from ninth to fifth. Yin month with southern luck gives censorial office. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuchen / Gengshen:** Minister Lü Guangxun, without sons. **Wuyin / Jiayin:** a military commander, without sons. **Jiazi / Wuchen:** a senior provincial administrator. **Xinsi / Jiawu:** a prefect. **Gengchen / Dinghai:** Shen Tonghe, metropolitan-examination first in Bingchen; dismissed and banished to frontier service for fraudulent writing. **Jiaxu / Bingyin:** a provincial graduate.〉[^155]

**Guisi day.** Zi-month birth in a southern region of clear mountains and beautiful waters gives high distinction. Zi and Wei in year and month give substantial wealth; Xu and Mao close attendance; Wei, Xu, and Shen a scholarly office. 〈Recorded cases—**Guihai / Bingchen:** Qiu Luan, Marquis of Xianning; his coffin was broken open and his severed head displayed. **Yiwei / Jichou:** a minister. **Guimao / Xinyou:** a marquis. **Jichou / Wuchen:** a provincial administration commissioner. **Xinchou / Xinchou:** a censor. **Jisi / Jisi:** Fu Xiaqi, metropolitan-examination first and bureau director, long-lived. **Renzi / Renyin:** a provincial graduate. **Bingchen / Renchen:** a provincial graduate. **Guiyou / Renxu:** a capital prefect.〉

**Guiwei day.** Elevated. Yin, Mao, and Wei months are ordinary; Chen, Xu, and Chou auspicious. Si month makes Wealth and Officer both flourish and gives distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Bingchen / Renchen:** an official in the Household Administration of the Heir Apparent. **Xinmao / Renchen:** an associate prefect. **Renyin / Xin–[branch missing]:** a junior provincial administrator. **Guihai / Renxu:** a chief surveillance commissioner, with many sons. **Xinmao / Renchen:** an assistant surveillance commissioner. **Jiachen / Yihai:** a provincial graduate. **Jisi / Bingyin:** Xiong Tingbi, military commissioner and vice minister. **Dingwei / Renzi:** two captions share the flattened record: Minister Wang Daoheng of Wuyuan, successful in Jimao and Guiwei; and Deputy Court Minister Lin Xuezeng of Jinjiang, successful in Renchen. **Dingyou / Wushen:** the Prince of Chu. **Renxu / Wushen:** a senior provincial administrator.〉[^156]

**Guiyou day.** Solitary distinction. Si, You, and Chou months give distinction; Yin and Mao are inauspicious; Zi and Wu give wealth; Xu month great distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Wushen / Gengshen:** Master Han Wengong. **Dinghai / Gengxu:** Minister Qin Fengshan. **Dingsi / Gengxu:** a provincial graduate. **Jiayin / Wuchen:** Minister Lin Han. **Jiaxu / Renshen:** a tribute student admitted under the special regulations. **Dingchou / Guichou:** wealth. **Xinhai / Jihai:** Metropolitan Graduate Rao Wei. **Yichou / Yiyou:** Governor Li Bing. **Xinhai / Jihai:** a court minister. **Dingmao / Renzi:** early death.〉

**Guihai day.** No Jia Wood in the pillars to break and damage the Officer Storehouse gives distinction; luck goes south. 〈Recorded cases—**Bingzi / Xinmao:** a privy councillor. **Yihai / Dinghai:** military distinction, with much misfortune. **Dingmao / Wushen:** a marquis. **Gengxu / Renwu:** wealth develops, followed by violent death.〉

〈Jade Emerging from Lantian:  
Do not resent its late appearance.  
The key encounters Mao and Xu;  
Wealth and Lu follow from Heaven.〉

> A Gui day at Bingchen closes the Officer Storehouse;  
> Wealth Star is exposed, yet has no qi.  
> Officer needs the key; Wealth needs to prosper:  
> Mao and [Xu] in the pillars bring distinction.

> A Gui day meeting Bingchen hour:  
> Wealth and Officer are locked inside the Storehouse.  
> Mao and [Xu] must appear for the key to open it;  
> Keeping the ancestral estate, the Six Relations obstruct.  
> Food God secretly lends assistance;  
> Amid emptiness and want, resources pass away in vain.  
> Poverty first, then wealth, is arranged within the fate;  
> Changing the ancestral establishment, renewed prosperity gradually brings ease.[^157]

## 54. Gui Days, Dingsi Hour
### 六癸日巳時斷
<!-- source: L2516-L2567 -->

> A Gui day born at Dingsi hour:  
> On noble ground, Wealth meets hidden Officer.  
> With support, behold Wealth and Lu flourishing;  
> Without a place to lean, blessing must be incomplete.

On a Gui day at Dingsi hour, Gui combines with Wealth and Officer. Gui takes Bing as Wealth, Wu as Officer, Geng as Seal, and Si as Tianyi Noble Person. Upon Si, Geng Metal is at Birth, Bing and Wu establish Lu, and Gui Water receives its embryo. Support and communication with the qi of a Water month give distinction; without communication with Water qi, the fate is ordinary. The hour meets the Three Wonders, and advancement generally comes in later life.

**Guichou day.** Poverty first, wealth afterward; Fire-and-Water luck brings advancement. 〈Recorded cases—**Guiyou / Guihai:** a prefect. **Xinsi / Jiawu:** a provincial graduate. **Gengxu / Jichou:** a deputy court minister. **Gengxu / Jiashen:** “Yi ministry,” designation uncertain. **Gengyin / Wuyin:** Hou Xun, minister of Revenue.〉

**Guimao day.** Zi month strengthens both body and Wealth, giving manifest distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Xinyou / Xinmao:** an assistant surveillance commissioner. **Gengshen / Dinghai:** a Secretariat drafter.〉

**Guisi day.** Wealth and Officer in Double Beauty. Zi month gives distinction; a Fire month riches. Earth exposed in the heavenly stems, with Wu and Wei in the branches, gives great authority and distinction. Pure Chou gives the highest rank. 〈One account says:〉 distinction amid low station. 〈Another:〉 death through bloodshed amid distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Gengchen / Dinghai:** Minister Liu Zhang. **Wuwu / Jiwei:** Minister Weng Wanda, an eminent minister. **Xinmao / Xinchou:** Academician Xu Gao. **Yichou / Jichou:** Vice Minister Li Dai. **Wuchen / Xinyou:** a capital prefect. **Bingxu / Jiawu:** minister of the Court of State Ceremonial.〉

**Guiwei day.** Yin, Wu, and Xu months make body and Wealth flourish and become manifest. Autumn and winter strengthen Lu but bring injury and disability. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingmao / Xinhai:** Chief Censor Lu Keyou. **Jiawu / Dingmao:** Metropolitan Graduate Chen Kezhai. **Dingyou / Wushen:** a provincial graduate.〉

**Guiyou day.** Poverty first, wealth afterward. Si and You months make Officer and Seal both flourish; Hai, Mao, and Wei give Food and Hurting Officer Generating Wealth, with manifest distinction. Wu and Xu strengthen Wealth and are also auspicious. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiawu / Jisi:** Minister Li Jie. **Xinmao / Xinmao:** a deputy court minister. **Xinsi / Yiwei:** a prefect. **Renzi / Xinhai:** Yang Weicong, palace-examination first. **Bingzi / Dingyou:** a deputy general. **Gengzi / Yiyou:** a deputy surveillance commissioner. **Xinmao / Xinmao:** Liu Jian, palace-examination second. **Xinchou / Dingyou:** a senior provincial administrator.〉[^158]

**Guihai day.** Punishment and harm. Spring- and summer-month births are good; autumn’s Seal-Ribbon is auspicious; winter ordinary. Gengyin in year and month gives military distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Xinwei / Wuxu:** a senior provincial administrator. **Jiazi / Dingmao:** a prefect. **Renzi / Jiyou:** a censor. **Dingmao / Yisi:** a provincial graduate. **Yiwei / Renwu:** Chief Censor Wang Xuan. **Jiawu / Bingzi:** a prefect.〉

〈A Phoenix Alighting on Mount Jing:  
Beautiful jade lies hidden in the stone.  
Aspiration fulfilled, one meets a fine craftsman:  
If not noble, then amply rich.〉

> At Si hour, Lu and Horse together strive to lead;  
> Impartial creation brings forth a great worthy.  
> Punishment and clash halve the blessing; let there be no Void or control.  
> Luck arrives, and the name resounds through the Nine Heavens.

> A Gui day approaching Dingsi hour:  
> Noble Person, Lu, and Horse are in One Country.  
> Threefold Snake and Horse set the court’s order aright;  
> The person passes through jade halls and golden steps.  
> Ren, Hai, Shen, and Yin diminish the blessing by half;  
> Fear only luck falling into Void.  
> If truly there is no clash, control, punishment, or injury,  
> One bows in ceremonial dance beside the Phoenix Pool.[^159]

## 55. Gui Days, Wuwu Hour
### 六癸日午時斷
<!-- source: L2568-L2607 -->

> A Gui day born at Wuwu hour:  
> Transformation into Fire approaches the hour’s Imperial Prosperity.  
> Luck welcomes eastern and southern Wood-and-Fire ground;  
> The official is pure and upright, Lu honored and flourishing.

On a Gui day at Wuwu hour, transformed qi completes a Fire configuration. Gui combines with Wu and transforms into Fire; at Wu, Imperial Prosperity joins the configuration and gives [distinction]. If the body is strong and does not transform, the qi of Gui Water’s region is drawn south, leaving Gui Water without qi: distinction, but a short lifespan. Eastern luck is auspicious.[^160]

**Guichou day.** Substantial wealth. Southern luck gives distinction but a short life; eastern luck is auspicious. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingwei / Bingwu:** a metropolitan graduate. **Dingmao / Bingwu:** a provincial graduate. **Yihai / Dinghai:** a county magistrate.〉

**Guimao day.** Shen, Zi, Chen, and Hai months strengthen the body, preventing transformation: an ordinary fate. Mao and Xu months give distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Bingshen / Yiwei:** Vice Minister Gu Dongjie. **Gengshen / Guiwei:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jiawu / Renshen:** a county magistrate.〉

**Guisi day.** Great wealth in middle life. Eastern luck gives manifest distinction; Shen and Wei months also give distinction. 〈Recorded case—**Jiaxu / Bingyin:** wealth and distinction.〉

**Guiwei day.** Yin, Wu, and Xu transform into Fire and complete the configuration, giving manifest distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Renwu / Xinhai:** a censor. **Guihai / Jiayin:** a supervising secretary. **Renxu / Wushen:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jiawu / Jisi:** a senior provincial administrator. **Guiyou / Guihai:** an official’s son. **Renshen / Bingwu:** Censor Zhou Qiyuan. **Dinghai / Renyin:** a senior provincial administrator. **Jiyou / Gengchen:** a metropolitan graduate and assistant instructor.〉

**Guiyou day.** Injury to wife and household wealth, with a beginning but no conclusion. A Zi year gives a distinguished child but no Lu. Southern luck is good. 〈Another account says:〉 ruin of the ancestral establishment and ominous defeat. 〈Recorded cases—**Dinghai / Gengxu:** Minister Jin Ze. **Gengshen / Wuyin:** Lin Yuntong, left censor-in-chief. **Yimao / Xinsi:** a left censor-in-chief. **Dingsi / Renzi:** a capital prefect. **Jihai / Bingzi:** a county magistrate. **Yiyou / Gengchen:** a metropolitan graduate. **Renyin / Jiachen:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jiwei / Jiaxu:** a censor. **Dingmao / Wushen:** “Jia ministry,” designation uncertain.〉

**Guihai day.** Distinction. Wei month with eastern luck, or a Metal month with restraint, both give manifest distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Wushen / Gengshen:** the highest distinction. **Guihai / Guihai:** blind in both eyes. **Guihai / Yimao:** first-rank title through a son. **Yisi / Jiashen:** a provincial graduate stripped of status. **Jiayin / Jiaxu:** a metropolitan graduate. **Wuyin / Wuwu:** a provincial graduate.〉[^161]

〈General Star Supporting Lu:  
Flourishing Wealth generates Officer and Lu.  
Clash, breaking, and combination make it ordinary;  
Running hither and thither, Wealth is insufficient.〉

> General Star supports Lu, though fates are high or low;  
> To be favored by others brings both right and wrong.  
> Purpose fulfilled, a moulting rooster becomes a phoenix;  
> A tiger lying on a level slope is bullied by rabbits.

> A Gui day meeting Wuwu hour:  
> The Heavenly Origins face Achieving Completion.  
> Transformation into True Fire displays majestic light;  
> Calamities are dispelled, and blessing endures.  
> Ren meeting Jiayin diminishes it by half;  
> Disputes, success, and failure are hard to guard against.  
> The Six Relations are discordant, with hidden punishment and injury;  
> Abundant, flourishing resources are hard to obtain.

## 56. Gui Days, Jiwei Hour
### 六癸日未時斷
<!-- source: L2608-L2647 -->

> A Gui day born at [Jiwei] hour:  
> Ghost flourishes, the body declines, and blessing is uneven.  
> The month’s qi does not communicate; no rescue comes.  
> Ordinary food and clothing suffer further loss.[^162]

On a Gui day at Jiwei hour, ghost flourishes and the body declines. Gui takes Ji as ghost. The two Ji, visible and hidden upon Wei, sit in their own position; Gui Water lacks qi. Turbid and unclear, success and failure alternate. Even with restraint through the month, if Water qi does not communicate, the fate is ordinary. Gui belongs to the kidneys, paired with the bladder as its bowel; there are disorders of the lower back, knees, and lower body, or abscesses and growths along the pathways traversed by the kidney channel.[^163]

**Guichou day.** Elevated. Both year and month Chou give poverty and deprivation. Chen, Xu, Chou, and Wei strengthen Earth, indicating eye disorders, poverty, and low station. Communication with Metal, Wood, and Water qi gives distinction. Wu month with northeast luck gives sixth- or seventh-rank distinction. 〈Another account says:〉 punishment and breaking. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiachen / Jiaxu:** a commander-in-chief. **Renchen / Bingwu:** a provincial graduate. **Bingzi / Bingshen:** Grand Secretary Xu’s titled wife.〉[^164]

**Guimao day.** Zi, Si, and Wei in year and month give second rank. Si and Yin bring injury to the Six Relations through punishment. 〈Recorded cases—**Renzi / Dingwei:** Minister Zhang Jing, with an ominous end. **Dingsi / Renzi:** a senior provincial administrator. **Renxu / Gengxu:** a metropolitan graduate.〉

**Guisi day.** Wealth and Officer in Double Beauty. Without Wu Earth in the pillars, and with Mao Wood combining into the configuration, judge Indirect Officer upon the Hour under restraint: distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuyin / Gengyin:** a provincial administration commissioner. **Wuchen / Yimao:** a censor. **Gengwu / Guiwei:** an assistant prefect. **Guiwei / Jiwei:** a prefectural judge. **Guihai / Wuwu:** Xu Keqiu, governor of Sichuan.〉

**Guiwei day.** Elevated. Spring gives Indirect Officer under restraint and is auspicious; summer ordinary; autumn and winter strengthen the body. Shen month with Wood luck gives manifest distinction; Si and Chen years give office in one of the six ministries. 〈Another account says:〉 punishment and injury. 〈Recorded cases—**Renxu / Wushen:** Vice Minister Ye Tang. **Jimao / Dingmao:** a metropolitan graduate.〉

**Guiyou day.** Yin, Si, Shen, You, Chou, and Xu in year and month give distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Renyin / Dingwei:** Minister Mao Bowen. **Wuyin / Jiayin:** a provincial graduate.〉

**Guihai day.** Distinction. Wei-month birth gives loyalty and filial piety together, with office in the Censorate. Mixed Killings and Officer raise concern that life will end away from home. Hai in year and month gives distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingmao / Dingwei:** Grand Secretary Li Dongyang. **Bingzi / Yiwei:** a metropolitan graduate. **Gengchen / Dinghai:** an assistant prefect. **Guichou / Gengshen:** a senior provincial administrator.〉

〈Bitterness Ends and Sweetness Returns:  
Before the time is met, one suffers restless toil.  
Youth finds fulfillment difficult;  
Leaving the ancestral home permits a household to be established.〉

> Indirect Officer, the hidden ghost, lies buried in the Storehouse;  
> Danger, alarm, and sorrow prevent Wealth from gathering.  
> Chou and Xu meet: the key is auspicious.  
> Blessing developed in flourishing is surely free of calamity.

> A Gui day approaching Jiwei hour:  
> Within the Storehouse, ghost depletes the declining body.  
> Unless Mao and Xu come with the key to open it,  
> Locked shut, it cannot find peace or passage.  
> Fallen flowers bloom again and bear fruit;  
> Parents and the ranks of geese find little harmony.  
> Even so, poverty first gives way to wealth:  
> Cast off the old, welcome the new, without obstruction.[^165]

## 57. Gui Days, Gengshen Hour
### 六癸日申時斷
<!-- source: L2648-L2687 -->

> A Gui day born at Gengshen hour:  
> Officer Star and flourishing Seal occupy its branch.  
> With neither Ji, Bing, Yin, nor Si in the pillars,  
> There will be a time of splendor, wealth, and distinction.

On a Gui day at Gengshen hour, judge Exclusive Seal Combining Lu. Gui takes Wu as Direct Officer and Geng as Direct Seal. Upon Shen, Geng flourishes and Wu is at Birth. Shen combines with Wu and Bing within Si, giving the Gui day Wealth and Officer. Support, no Wealth in the pillars, and no breaking, harm, punishment, or clash injuring Officer and Seal give distinction. Wealth in the pillars and travel through Wealth luck produce advances and retreats, with little distinction.[^166]

**Guichou day.** Chen, Xu, Chou, and Wei in year and month give literary talent surpassing the age and office of third rank. Zi month seats the body upon Lu and is also auspicious. 〈Recorded cases—**Yiyou / Guiwei:** a chancellor. **Gengzi / Wuzi:** Vice Minister Han Huai. **Wushen / Jiayin:** a vice minister. **Bingshen / Yiwei:** a commander-in-chief. **Gengxu / Jimao:** a senior provincial administrator. **Jiwei / Jiaxu:** a senior provincial administrator. **Wuxu / Yichou:** a deputy commissioner. **Renwu / Gengxu:** a vice bureau director. **Guiyou / Yichou:** an associate prefect.〉

**Guimao day.** Mao month forms Combining Lu; northwest luck brings distinction, gold, and purple. Shen month with northeast luck gives censorial office. Gengchen or Gengxu in year and month forms Direct Officer and gives distinction. Chen month with Metal-and-Water luck gives seventh rank. 〈Recorded cases—**Guichou / Gengshen:** Minister Han. **Jiawu / Jiaxu:** Chief Censor Sheng Yingqi. **Yimao / Jiashen:** a guard commander.〉

**Guisi day.** Poverty, yet this too is Combining Lu. Wei-month birth gives accomplishment in learning; northwest luck gives distinction, as does northeast luck. 〈Recorded cases—**Xinyou / Bingshen:** a vice minister. **Wuzi / Bingzi:** received an honorific patent; a registrar shared the chart. **Jiawu / Xinwei:** a provincial graduate. **Dingyou / Bingwu:** office in the two provincial commissions.〉

**Guiwei day.** Jiayin and Yimao in the pillars let Ji combine with Jia and Yi with Geng: a worthy wife and filial children, with splendor coming later. You and Shen in year and month give third or fourth rank. Autumn birth with Wood-and-Fire luck gives second or third rank; Hai and Mao give advancement through letters. 〈Recorded cases—**Yiyou / Guiwei:** a chancellor. **Yichou / Bingxu:** a deputy court minister. **Yiwei / Yiyou:** a metropolitan graduate.〉

**Guiyou day.** Mao, You, Xu, and Yin in year and month give distinction. Shen and You with Fire-and-Wood luck give great distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Gengwu / Yiyou:** a chancellor. **Gengxu / Wuzi:** a commander-in-chief. **Gengxu / Renwu:** a commander-in-chief. **Renshen / Renzi:** a provincial graduate.〉

**Guihai day.** An even temperament, a solitary person. Mao and Wei months give a learned and noble fate; Shen also gives distinction. Zi and Shen give authority in close attendance. 〈One account says:〉 poor in youth, fond of learning, and capable. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingchou / Renzi:** a vice minister. **Guimao / Gengshen:** distinction. **Yiyou / Yiyou:** distinction. **Jiaxu / Bingzi:** a judicial reviewer. **Guiwei / Yichou:** an assistant prefect.〉

〈The Golden Crow, the Sun:  
It soars aloft toward the Nine Heavens.  
With no control or breaking in the pillars,  
Its reputation commands the imperial court.〉

> Examine closely the Gui day at Gengshen;  
> Deep within Yu’s Gate, a dragon is seen in flight.  
> Literary talent gains assistance and majestic force;  
> The pillars combine Wealth and Officer—rare in the world.

> A Gui day at Gengshen hour is the proper form;  
> Seal-Ribbon joins Officer Star in combination.  
> Hai, Yin, Shen, Bing, and Si bring punishment and clash;  
> Parting and joining leave one’s establishment unsettled.  
> Without breaking, the yellow examination list displays the surname;  
> The common person’s Wealth and Lu are peaceful.  
> If truly there are no punishments, harms, or baleful stars,  
> The brocade-feathered fowl becomes a phoenix.[^167]

## 58. Gui Days, Xinyou Hour
### 六癸日酉時斷
<!-- source: L2688-L2731 -->

> A Gui day born at Xinyou hour:  
> The body loses its ground—what more can it possess?  
> Visible and hidden in the branch, Xin inflicts injury;  
> Without assistance, profit and fame never come to fulfillment.

On a Gui day at Xinyou hour, Owl Spirit is both visible and hidden. Gui sees Xin as Inverted Food. Xin establishes its flourishing position upon You, while Gui loses its ground. Without support or rescue, livelihood must be sought through meeting the noble; with support, the result is auspicious.[^168]

**Guichou day.** An autumn month gives the Seal-Ribbon configuration, auspicious in Officer-and-Killings luck. Winter gives blessing and Lu together. Zi and Yin in year and month, with neither Wu nor Ji in the exposed stems, give distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Yichou / Jichou:** a vice minister. **Xinyou / Xinmao:** Feng Jing, palace-examination first. **Jiazi / Bingyin:** an assistant director in a government directorate. **Jihai / Renshen:** Tang Jiujing, metropolitan graduate and county magistrate. **Dingsi / Bingwu:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jiashen / Yisi:** “Yi ministry,” designation uncertain.〉

**Guimao day.** Day Nobility. Chen, Xu, Chou, and Wei months give distinction; Zi and Mao in year and month give great distinction; Si and Wu also give distinction. 〈Another account says:〉 unfavorable to wife and children. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingyou / Guimao:** the Ming Muzong emperor; another account gives Bingchen hour. Reigned seven years, aged thirty-seven. **Xinyou / Gengzi:** a commander-in-chief. **Gengzi / Jimao:** Grand Secretary Yan Song. This old man had no son; Shifan was his wife’s nephew. The great calamity that befell him late in life was all caused by this boy. What a pity! Yet in my judgment there is nothing to pity. **Renwu / Guichou:** Honorary Official Liu of Shiqiu [place-name uncertain], with two sons, Yuanzhen and Yuanlin.〉[^169]

**Guisi day.** Solitary. Yin or Mao year with Chen, Xu, Chou, or Wei month gives distinction. Zi, Wu, Mao, and You give moderate distinction; Yin, Shen, Si, and Hai the greatest. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiashen / Yihai:** a ministry secretary. **Xinsi / Jihai:** Palace Eunuch Feng Bao. Water is defeated at You; the hour governs the final outcome.〉[^170]

**Guiwei day.** Spring gives poverty; summer difficulty followed by ease; autumn is auspicious; winter distinguished. 〈Recorded cases—**Jihai / Bingzi:** a bureau director. **Jiazi / Jisi:** an academician. **Jiayin / Guiyou:** an administrative secretary who lived thirty-five years. **Renchen / Jiyou:** a ministry secretary.〉

**Guiyou day.** Shen and You months give much Seal-Ribbon: the ancestral establishment can be preserved, but the wife’s household is damaged. Zi and Si in year and month, with Geng and Xin exposed, complete Lu, Noble Person, and Seal-Ribbon: distinction beyond words. Yin and Xu bring gold at the waist; Wu and Hai great distinction. Chou month makes Killings heavy, with misfortune and early death. 〈Recorded cases—**Gengzi / Xinsi:** the Ming Chengzu emperor. **Wuwu / Gengshen:** a privy councillor. **Renzi / Xinhai:** a provincial administration commissioner. **Jiazi / Yihai:** a bureau director. **Jihai / Xinwei:** a censor.〉

**Guihai day.** Xu month with southeast luck brings gold and purple; northeast luck gives censorial office. Zi month establishes Lu; Wealth and Officer encountered in the year give great distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Guihai / Xinyou:** the Ming Shenzong emperor, who held the realm for forty-eight years. **Wuyin / Jiazi:** Hanlin scholar Ni Qizuo. **Renwu / Renzi:** a deputy commissioner.〉

〈Wild Geese Losing Their Flock:  
Kinfolk scatter east and west.  
The Six Relations offer little support;  
Keeping the ancestral estate does not establish the lineage.〉

> Gui is the Heavenly Origin, Xinyou the hour;  
> Every resource of the mind is spent in daily haste.  
> Officer and Seal generate each other, and Seal finds combination;  
> Though rich in the breast, the life remains ordinary.

> A Gui day meeting Xinyou hour:  
> Inverted Food and Indirect Seal are hard to restrain.  
> Without support in the pillars, accept poverty for now;  
> Wealth and Officer met in the month are also fitting.  
> Fear only Gui Water losing its ground:  
> Unable to harness and direct, how can it bear the burden?  
> The Six Relations, flesh and blood, scatter east and west:  
> A fate of lifelong labor and hardship.

## 59. Gui Days, Renxu Hour
### 六癸日戌時斷
<!-- source: L2732-L2775 -->

> A Gui day born at Renxu hour:  
> Within the branch, Direct Officer arises in Wealth’s Storehouse.  
> The month providing rescue, distinction often comes to completion;  
> Without support, riches never come.

On a Gui day at Renxu hour, Water and Fire Achieve Completion. Gui takes Bing and Ding as Wealth and Wu Earth as Officer. Wu combines with Gui and flourishes, giving intelligence and resourcefulness. Communication with the month’s qi and support give distinction; without communication, the fate is ordinary. Communication with a Fire or Earth month’s qi gives wealth and distinction together; communication through luck is also auspicious.

**Guichou day.** Punishment. Hai month in a region of substantial Earth gives the highest distinction. Chen and Shen in year and month, with southern luck, give first place in the palace examination. The fifth month with southern luck gives censorial office; spring or autumn birth with southern luck gives eighth or ninth rank. 〈Recorded cases—**Gengwu / Wuyin:** a chief councillor. **Xinsi / Wuxu:** an assistant surveillance commissioner. **Gengzi / Wuzi:** Jiao Hong, palace-examination first.〉

**Guimao day.** Day Nobility. Yin and Si in year and month, with Wu and Ding exposed, make Wealth and Officer flourish: great distinction and authority. Mao, Chen, Chou, Wu, Zi, and similar years and months give literary distinction. You or Xu with Metal-and-Earth luck gives fifth or sixth rank. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuyin / Dingsi:** Yu Qian, Master Zhongsu. **Renwu / Guichou:** an assistant surveillance commissioner. **Renchen / Renyin:** a county magistrate. **Dingsi / Bingwu:** a provincial graduate. **Yimao / Renshen:** a provincial graduate.〉

**Guisi day.** Wealth and Officer in Double Beauty. Spring is ordinary; summer, autumn, and winter distinguished. Chen, Chou, Wei, Yin, and You in year and month give the headship of the Censorate. 〈Recorded cases—**Guiyou / Jiayin:** a chief censor. **Jiwei / Dingchou:** Chief Censor Chen Shixian. **Xinyou / Gengzi:** a metropolitan graduate. **Gengwu / Dinghai:** the wife of Grand Tutor Yang and mother of the vice minister; died in Renwu year. **Renshen / Gengxu:** “Jia ministry,” designation uncertain. **Wuchen / Yimao:** Zhang Yicheng, palace-examination first; died in Bingchen year.〉[^171]

**Guiwei day.** Punishment. Si-month birth gives third or fourth rank. Zi and Geng in year and month give distinction in close attendance. 〈Recorded cases—**Bingyin / Guisi:** Minister Lin Tingji. **Jiayin / Gengwu:** a provincial graduate. **Bingchen / Xinchou:** great wealth.〉

**Guiyou day.** Hai and Zi months give talent, intelligence, high distinction, a worthy wife, and filial children. Spring is ordinary; summer’s Wealth and Officer and autumn’s Seal-Ribbon are all auspicious. Chen and Chou punish and clash with Xu’s Storehouse, completing wealth and distinction. Xu month with southeast luck gives military distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Guisi / Renxu:** Grand Secretary Hong Chengchou. **Xinmao / Bingshen:** a right provincial administration commissioner. **Renshen / Wushen:** a metropolitan graduate. **Renshen / Bingwu:** a provincial graduate.〉

**Guihai day.** Spring birth gives Hurting Officer Meeting Officer; summer strengthens Wealth; autumn and winter are auspicious, with accomplishment in name and profit. Xu or Chen month with Hai-and-Zi luck gives distinction; Zi month with southwest luck gives gold and purple. 〈Recorded cases—**Xinwei / Gengzi:** Minister He Weibai. **Yihai / Dinghai:** Education Commissioner Jin Lijing. **Jiazi / Dingchou:** a censor. **Guimao / Jiazi:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jimao / Dingmao:** a regional military commander. **Jiachen / Bingzi:** a censor. **Guiwei / Guihai:** “Jia ministry,” designation uncertain.〉

〈A Field Mouse in the Ship’s Granary:  
In ill-favored luck, endure restless travel for now.  
The key meets Chen and Chou;  
Later prospects bring much blessing and wealth.〉

> Tianyi, Ren, and Gui are arrayed at Xu hour;  
> Wealth and Officer within the Storehouse await the key.  
> Without punishment and clash, the lock remains closed in vain;  
> Youth finds advancement difficult and calamity arises anew.

> A Gui day meeting Renxu hour:  
> Within it, stores and treasuries are full.  
> General Star and Heavenly Virtue support one another;  
> Chen and Xu are keys that open and assist.  
> Earth flourishes in the Long-Flowing-Water configuration;  
> Among the Six Relations, kindness turns to estrangement.  
> Without Void, there is increase and surplus;  
> Middle and later life enjoy splendor and blessing.[^172]

## 60. Gui Days, Guihai Hour
### 六癸日亥時斷
<!-- source: L2776-L2825 -->

> A Gui day born at Guihai hour:  
> Lu and Horse Fly Heavenward, approaching a flourishing influence.  
> Neither Officer Star nor entangling bonds appear:  
> Surely an exceptional person within a noble configuration.

On a Gui day at Guihai hour, there is the Lu and Horse Flying Heavenward configuration. Gui Water is vigorous upon Hai. Gui takes Wu as Officer and Bing as Wealth, but Bing and Wu are both extinguished within Hai, leaving Gui without Wealth or Officer. Hai instead clashes forth Bing and Wu within Si, which fly toward Gui as Wealth and Officer. With no Wu or Ji in the pillars to entangle it, and no Officer Star to break Lu, Geng and Xin appearing give purity and refinement. The person is intelligent and resourceful, with distinction in provincial authority.[^173]

**Guichou day.** Arching Lu. Si, You, and Chou births give Blessing, Virtue, and Refined Qi. Wu month is ordinary; Mao and You months with southern luck give gold and purple and censorial office, but long life is difficult. 〈Recorded cases—**Renzi / Guichou:** a minister. **Guimao / Guihai:** Vice Minister Xu Chengming. **Guiwei / Xinyou:** a metropolitan graduate. **Guihai / Guihai:** a supervising secretary and a chief censor shared the chart. **Guisi / Jiayin:** Grand Secretary Zhang Wei. **Xinwei / Xinchou:** a provincial graduate.〉

**Guimao day.** Ordinary Day Nobility. In Yin and Mao months, judge Hurting Officer; Metal-and-Water luck gives censorial office. Chen, Xu, Chou, and Wei months strengthen Officer. Pure Mao gives third-rank distinction; Chen and Xu punishing Mao indicate solitude and poverty. 〈Recorded cases—**Renshen / Wushen:** Tang Ruyi, palace-examination first. **Jichou / Yihai:** a deputy commissioner. **Jiashen / Dingmao:** a metropolitan graduate. **Dingwei / Renyin:** a commander-in-chief. **Gengwu / Yiyou:** a supervising secretary. **Yimao / Renchen:** a prefect. **Bingshen / Gengyin:** a mobile-force commander.〉[^174]

**Guisi day.** Chou month gives Mixed-Qi Seal-Ribbon and distinction. Hai and Zi in year and month give distinction in southern luck. This is also Lu and Horse in One Country; Si and Shen in year and month give great distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Guisi / Gengshen:** Grand Secretary Xu Zan. **Guimao / Jiayin:** a metropolitan graduate. **Renyin / Renyin:** a provincial graduate. **Jiayin / Xinchou:** an assistant court director. **Guimao / Guihai:** a metropolitan graduate. **Gengyin / Guiwei:** poor and vulgar. **Jihai / Yihai:** a magistrate, caption uncertain. **Jiazi / Bingyin:** a marquis.〉

**Guiwei day.** Wealth and Officer sit beneath the body. Chen, Xu, Chou, and Wei months give distinction in northeast luck. An autumn month with eastern luck gives seventh or eighth rank; Direct Seal and Direct Officer exposed in the stems bring gold and purple. 〈Recorded cases—**Jisi / Gengwu:** a Hanlin scholar. **Gengyin / Gengwu:** a supervising secretary. **Guichou / Gengshen:** an assistant prefect. **Dingchou / Dingwei:** first in the provincial examination. **Bingshen / Wuxu:** Minister Li Wen, from Renqiu; a solitary, impoverished man from Suzhou shared the chart. **Jisi / Jiaxu:** Governor Li Yun.〉

**Guiyou day.** Chen, Xu, Chou, and Wei months give a livelihood answering the heart’s desires. You month with northeast luck gives eighth- or ninth-rank distinction; Shen month with eastern luck gives fifth rank. 〈Recorded cases—**Guichou / Jiwei:** Hanlin scholar Zhang Sixiu. **Gengzi / Dinghai:** a minister. **Renchen / Renzi:** a commander of ten thousand households. **Bingxu / Dingyou:** an academician. **Xinwei / Wuxu:** a metropolitan graduate. **Bingzi / Bingshen:** a provincial graduate. **Guiwei / Jiayin:** a provincial graduate. **Jiazi / Xinwei:** a provincial graduate. **Guimao / Jiazi:** a junior provincial administrator. **Jiachen / Renshen:** a provincial graduate. **Wuwu / Bingchen:** a circuit official in the southern administration.〉

**Guihai day.** Intelligent and untrammeled in spirit, with great wealth in middle life. Winter-month birth gives Flying Heavenly Lu and Horse; without Wu, Ji, or Zi filling the position or entangling it, there is manifest distinction. Otherwise, solitude and injury; as a monk or Daoist, purity and loftiness are also indicated. Chen and Hai in year and month, with Xin and Ren exposed and no Si filling the position, give intelligence, breadth of mind, and great distinction. Mao month brings gold and purple; Si and Chou also give distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Renchen / Xinhai:** Minister Wang Shouren, enfeoffed as Earl of Xinjian. **Yiyou / Jimao:** Education Commissioner Zong Chen, praised as a gifted writer, who lived thirty-six years and had no sons. **Guichou / Dingsi:** a junior provincial administrator. **Jimao / Jisi:** a provincial administrator. **Bingwu / Gengzi:** a prefect. **Guichou / Guihai:** Chen, palace-examination first. **Bingxu / Yiwei:** Assistant Surveillance Commissioner Liu Shiqiu, short-lived, with nine sons. **Dingsi / Xinhai:** a vice minister. **Guiyou / Jiayin:** a vice director of the Department of State Affairs. **Xinchou / Xinchou:** a county magistrate. **Jichou / Bingzi:** Zhou Yanru, metropolitan- and palace-examination first in Guichou, twice admitted to the Grand Secretariat, ordered to die in Guiwei year.〉[^175]

〈The Oriole Ascending to a Tall Tree:  
Turning from darkness, it returns toward light.  
The key encounters control and breaking;  
Await the wings’ ascent in flight.〉

> Repeated yin Water penetrates the ocean waves;  
> Youth, not yet favored, lets the years slip by.  
> A constrained dragon transforms only when its purpose is fulfilled;  
> Before its time arrives, the tiger lies upon the slope.

> A Gui day meeting Guihai hour:  
> Defeated Wealth carries Lu to open passage.  
> Autumn and summer are welcome, spring and winter avoided;  
> Wu, Bing, Geng, and Wood bring riches and abundance.  
> Si, Hai, Jia, and Bing bring reversals;  
> The Six Relations are deeply discordant.  
> The person never settles, delighting in change and upheaval:  
> A fate of defeat first and accomplishment afterward.[^176]

<!-- source: L2826-L2832 -->
Of the foregoing fates, six parts in ten belonged to the original text, and four parts in ten have been added. Occasionally one fate is recorded in two places. Could one say there are no errors of hearsay? What matters is that the person who understands fate weigh their import and judge the waxing and waning accordingly.[^177]

*End of Sanming Tonghui, juan nine.*

〈Siku classification: Masters Division; Arts of Calculation; Fate Books and Physiognomy; *Sanming Tonghui*.〉

## Translator’s Endnotes

[^01]: **Controlling text and witnesses.** **S** denotes the repository’s [Chinese juan nine](../source/juan-09.md), exported from [Wikisource revision 761700](https://zh.wikisource.org/w/index.php?oldid=761700); its checksum appears in the header. **G616**, **G617**, **G618**, and **G619** denote the parallel *Sanming tonghui* extracts in the *Gujin Tushu Jicheng*, Arts juan [616, revision 1942579](https://zh.wikisource.org/w/index.php?oldid=1942579), [617, revision 1942580](https://zh.wikisource.org/w/index.php?oldid=1942580), [618, revision 1942581](https://zh.wikisource.org/w/index.php?oldid=1942581), and [619, revision 1942582](https://zh.wikisource.org/w/index.php?oldid=1942582). **U** is the shorter [unlabelled Wikisource juan nine, revision 2613372](https://zh.wikisource.org/w/index.php?oldid=2613372). Comparison is selective, not a comprehensive collation of historical facsimiles. G’s volume pages transclude separately editable Page texts: a parent revision does not freeze all those underlying transcriptions. These are parallel witnesses, not additional Siku editions; agreement alone does not establish independent descent. The Siku transcription governs coverage and sequence, including its additional catalogue material. All English numbering is editorial. The separate Siku editorial preface remains untranslated.

[^02]: **Continuity with juan eight.** The reference units are day–hour combinations, supplemented by the year and month of each recorded example. The six occurrences of a stem are not six consecutive days. 官, 殺/煞, 印, 食, and related capitalized expressions retain the established translations Officer, Killings, Seal, and Food; technical “body” commonly denotes the day stem and its strength. Directions in luck frequently denote branch/phase regions rather than a prescription to move geographically. 克 as an alleged injury to a spouse or child is the source’s prognosis, not verified personal culpability. Medical and violent-death assertions are historical material, not diagnoses or forecasts endorsed here. Unambiguous 己/已/巳 confusions are resolved contextually; substantial and doubtful changes are separately noted. Wu 戊 and Wu 午 are different signs despite their identical unaccented pinyin.

[^03]: **Flattened case grids.** S often prints a row of years and captions followed by the corresponding months; the translation restores readable year/month pairs, consulting G where needed. Names absent from S are not imported from G, nor are S’s additional cases removed. A shared-chart note may refer to more than one person, so caption counts do not count unique people. 刀筆 denotes legal or administrative drafting; 奔覓 indicates a restless search for livelihood. 進士, 舉人, and the several examination placings remain distinct. 潘春谷 is the appellation in S, retained without replacing it with an unprinted personal name.

[^04]: **A transmitted imperial annotation.** S calls the ruler 弘治, gives eighteen regnal years, and adds 丙寅年崩, death in Bingyin. G616 identifies him as 孝宗 and describes the reign but does not supply that death-year annotation. S’s date is retained as its statement, not independently certified chronology. The later imperial and biographical additions throughout these catalogues are not all necessarily the work of the original compiler.

[^05]: **Jiazi restored.** S labels this individual Jisi entry 庚子時, Gengzi hour, inside the Jiazi section. G616 reads 甲子時, supporting “[Jiazi].” The general heading and hour sequence also support it, but the repair is not based solely on a calculated expectation. Its favorable transformation statement is qualified by lack of public prominence and remains so.

[^06]: **Two charts explicitly ascribed to different textual accounts.** S places Shen Kun under Jisi/Jiazi with Gengshen/Wuzi and cites 星案, *Star Cases*; it also places him under Jiyou/Jiazi with Dingmao/Renzi and cites 原本, the original text. Both are retained without choosing a historically authenticated birth record. S’s Jiyou prohibition specifies 庚寅年, Gengyin year; G616 has 月, month. The translation preserves S’s year, visibly distinguished from the following months. The Jiwei paragraph’s favorable Yin/Hai and unfavorable Yin/Mao clauses likewise remain unreconciled.

[^07]: **The same year and month, different days.** S gives Sun Jigao as Jimao/Yichou in the account attributed to 星案 and Jiyou/Yichou in 原本. Both use Gengxu/Bingxu year and month. The two different day pillars are not silently merged; the textual attributions are essential evidence of variation within the transmitted catalogue.

[^08]: **A wife generating Wealth; a terse caption.** 妻生財可也 is kept as a wife generating wealth making the clash acceptable; its possible technical reference to the wife-position is not expanded into an invented rule. The final Bingxu/Xinmao caption is only 鄕㐫, here “a provincial case marked ominous.” It is too abbreviated to assign a definite examination degree or a particular violent outcome. The geese in the closing song represent siblings, not literal birds.

[^09]: **Wood restored from the parallel.** S has 亥子水水運, repeating Water; G616 has 水木運, Water-and-Wood luck. “[Wood]” follows that attested reading. S’s Dinghai/Guihai graduate differs from G616’s Dinghai/Guichou and remains unchanged. A parallel witness can support a damaged graph without replacing every differing but possible chart.

[^10]: **A damaged examination image.** S’s 虎標標名局 is supported by G616’s 虎榜標名局, a name entered on the tiger list. The bracketed “List” records the repair; it evokes examination success rather than a separate animal omen. 三台八位 is rendered Three Terraces and Eight Seats as the passage’s courtly/noble imagery; no extra natal pillars are being counted.

[^11]: **The annotator speaks.** The Song Kaichun caption says the speaker obtained the original *Sanming Tonghui* from him. This first-person remark is retained within the catalogue, not represented as a statement by the present translator. G616 lacks the caption. Its voice is evidence of a transmission or annotation layer; “original text” does not by itself establish possession of an authorial manuscript.

[^12]: **An unrepaired expression.** Both S and G616 transmit 波渣 in the final song. “Troubled waters” preserves the disturbance imagery; the familiar 波查, troubles or reversals, is a plausible interpretation, not an attested correction here. The verse’s adverse You/Geng/Xin clause stands beside the prose’s favorable restraint by Xin. Their disagreement is not resolved by deleting a negative or changing the identity of Seal and Owl.

[^13]: **An incomplete remonstrance annotation.** S breaks the caption after 諫 and resumes with 戊贈少卿. The remonstrance and honorary court appointment are intelligible; the isolated 戊 is not converted into an invented year or an asserted exile. G616 does not supply this case. 少卿 is a vice-ministerial court title, not necessarily vice minister of one of the Six Ministries.

[^14]: **Guiwei restored.** S gives the malformed 癸木 as Luo Yao’s year; G616 gives 癸未, supporting “[Guiwei].” The Fang Fengnian caption later in the section contains both a Renxu examination year and Bingxu death year: neither belongs to the natal year/month pair. The game in 善奕 is interpreted as go, retaining a skill caption rather than assigning an official rank.

[^15]: **A transmitted pairing left unchanged.** Both S and G616 say 壬申財官雙美, Renshen giving Wealth and Officer in Double Beauty. The verse is not rewritten to insert a different Officer stem on the strength of modern day-stem rules. 钱龍, the money-dragon, is a literary image of stored riches. 妻重 and 子晚 distinguish repeated marital attachments from the late establishment of offspring; they are not converted into exact numerical counts.

[^16]: **Si as Horse; two differing year readings.** 二巳通看 directs the reader to consider two Si together, not to treat Ji Earth itself as a branch. S gives Governor Li Zhi as 己酉/庚午; G616 has 乙酉/庚午. S’s possible Jiyou year remains. The additional Weng Zhengchun/clerk comparison, absent from G’s corresponding passage, is retained rather than omitted to match that witness.

[^17]: **Proper and lodged Lu.** S expressly distinguishes Ding’s 正祿 from Ji’s 寄祿, so Arching Lu is not presented as identical in every stem. The Gengzi graduate’s S month 癸木 is repaired to “[Guiwei]” with G616’s 癸未. Repeated or shared dates elsewhere are kept when they are valid transmitted pairs.

[^18]: **A wrong individual hour label.** S heads the Jihai entry 乙丑時, Yichou hour, although the section is Jisi. G616 gives 己巳時 and supplies the same Qian Rujing and following cases. “[Jisi]” is therefore a supported repair. 國師 is retained as state preceptor; the terse sentence does not justify inventing the holder of a specific historical religious office.

[^19]: **Household standing restored.** S reads 改名庭 in the final song; G616 has 改門庭, changing the household’s standing or establishment. The bracketed English follows 門. 戌亥與庚申 is retained as Xu, Hai, and Gengshen; this juxtaposition of separate branches and a complete pair is not converted into a new uniform list without evidence.

[^20]: **Concealed Brilliance, not a missing rule.** 伏晶 is translated as in juan eight. The ordinary Returning-Lu account and the alternative involving Jia and Bing are both present. The section’s paradoxical heading language—Lu returning, yet later turning away from Lu—belongs to its juxtaposed methods. It is not resolved by deleting the latter. The comparison with virtue enriching the body and wealth the house is literary praise, not a new calculational instruction.

[^21]: **An expressly marked missing judgment.** S has 己酉日庚午時〈原闕〉 and then the Ma Shiying case. The heading, lacuna notice, and case are all preserved. G616 passes from Jiwei directly to Jihai and does not recover this judgment. There are still six day positions in the reference section, but one has no surviving general verdict. The translation does not manufacture one from the other five.

[^22]: **Hidden Jia at Wei.** S and G616 both say 明辛暗甲, visible Xin and hidden Jia. This is not the usual list of stems stored inside Wei, but it is the transmitted explanation of Officer’s Storehouse and is not silently changed to Yi. S’s 不通主福, blessing without communication, differs from G616’s 主富, wealth; S governs the prose, while the opening verse’s wealth statement also remains.

[^23]: **The qualification is part of the record.** The repeated 一云 statements give adverse outcomes beside favorable main judgments: injury, shortened life, self-injury amid wealth, or loss amid distinction. These are retained as alternatives rather than transformed into a single favorable summary. Ji’s Birth at You belongs to the yin-stem life-stage account used here, not an error to be corrected to the yang Earth sequence.

[^24]: **Stored wealth and paired celestial images.** 貫朽粟陳 depicts wealth accumulated so long that coin-strings decay and grain ages. 乾清坤夷, Qian Clear and Kun Level, and the luan/phoenix upon wutong are transmitted names and images, not botanical or geographical prescriptions. S’s opening 若失天元 differs from G616’s 若失天時; Heavenly Origin is retained in the verse rather than silently substituted with season.

[^25]: **The catalogue’s judgment, not the translator’s.** S appends 狡猾, cunning, to Yu Dayou’s case. The word is translated without treating it as a verified moral assessment. The following alternate prognosis joins mental disturbance and kidney illness to great distinction; no modern diagnosis is inferred from the historical expressions.

[^26]: **Guiyou restored.** The Jichou entry in S has 癸亥時, Guihai hour; G616 gives 癸酉時, Guiyou, agreeing with the section and its cases. The bracketed correction is explicit. The prose calls Xin Food while individual entries also use Hurting-Officer configurations; those differing technical labels have not been made uniform by substitution.

[^27]: **Early death, not Heaven.** S’s Jisi/Renshen caption says 貴天; G616 reads 貴夭. “[Early death]” follows the supported 夭 reading. The Jianwen entry’s loss of the realm and wandering are S’s historical-prognostic account, not an independently verified narrative supplied by the translator.

[^28]: **An explicit conjectural repair.** S has 四十四支互換貴全; G616 has 四千四支互換貴全. Reading 四干四支, “four stems and four branches,” is a highly plausible repair of 十/千 to 干, but neither consulted transcription attests it cleanly. The supplied words are therefore bracketed. No forty-four-year lifespan or forty-four branches are invented from the damaged count.

[^29]: **A case before its individual heading.** S places the Renchen/Guimao censor after the general Jiaxu-hour explanation and before Jichou day. G616 explicitly assigns that pair to Jichou/Jiaxu and names a censor Fu. The translation preserves S’s placement, identifying it as such, rather than adding the name or silently moving the case. Readers reconstructing full charts should note the parallel’s assignment.

[^30]: **Zi month restored; shared cases retained.** S reads 子運木火運, Zi luck followed by Wood-and-Fire luck. G616 gives 子月, supporting “[month].” In the Jiwei catalogue G616 makes explicit that a ministry secretary and a provincial graduate share the Guiwei/Renxu pair with the bureau director; S lists all three captions, which are retained. In the final song 壬申丑神 is rendered Ren, Shen, and Chou spirit, without claiming the text uniquely determines whether the first two should instead be read as the whole Renshen pillar.

[^31]: **Wu Yiqin’s death and a shared chart.** S has 妖死, literally an uncanny or anomalous death; G616 reads 夭死, early death. “[Young]” follows the latter. S gives the fuller Shen Yingwen name and a Cheng from Shexian, whereas G’s shortened comparison omits those details. The name and shared-chart caption are retained without importing additional biographical information.

[^32]: **A partially lost month.** S leaves only 己 in the lower row beneath Jin Youzi’s Wushen year. G616 supplies 丁巳, Dingsi, supporting the bracketed month. The isolated 己 is thus not made into a free-standing month stem or a conjectural Jisi pillar. Li Xiya, Cui Dongzhou, and Cai Baishi are the appellations printed by S and are not silently replaced by personal names not given there.

[^33]: **Dates and a remaining lacuna.** The Yiwei/Wuyin case gives Yimao provincial and Jiaxu metropolitan examinations, administrative and salt-circuit service, and unfavorable Xu and Ren luck, then 原闕, a lacuna in the original. Those dates are not additional natal pillars. The Renchen/Yichou caption’s 諫坦 is interpreted as a remonstrance post; its death in the fourth month of Xinsi is kept as a separate event. No missing personal name or omitted career episode is invented.

[^34]: **Xinwei restored; alternative hour retained.** S’s 辛木 under Grand Secretary Li Ben is G617’s 辛未, supporting “[Xinwei].” Both give Renwu as an alternative hour. The corresponding Renwu-hour entry later writes 呂本, Lü Ben, rather than 李本; the translation keeps the two transmitted name-graphs and does not silently consolidate their biographies.

[^35]: **Identical pillars, differing circumstances.** The comparison of Huang Maoguan and Shen Jie preserves their different regions, order of death, and number of sons. These are the catalogue’s reports, not independently verified biographies. G617 writes 黃懋官 and supports reading S’s rare 𢡟 as Mao. The comparison is not normalized to any differently worded version elsewhere in the compilation.

[^36]: **A caption too short to complete.** S leaves 中 beside the Guichou/Guihai pair; the translation retains “zhong” as an incomplete caption rather than inventing a Secretariat office. G617 omits that case. S gives Lun Yixun both metropolitan first and palace second, whereas G’s shorter entry gives only metropolitan first; S’s fuller examination record is retained.

[^37]: **An unusual name retained.** Both S and G617 have 嚴蒙, Yan Meng. It has not been silently identified with or corrected to a better-known statesman. S’s Gengwu sentence 通土月氣貴不亦富 is compressed; the English construes it as distinction with communication and otherwise wealth, while G has the slightly different 不貴亦富, “if not distinguished, still rich.” The wording does not establish a single unconditional outcome.

[^38]: **A hostile judgment in the source.** The additional Huang Daozhou annotation calls him 假道學, a pretender to the Learning of the Way. This is translated as the annotator’s judgment, not endorsed as historical assessment. Gengchen court flogging and Bingxu death while commanding troops are career dates. The Lin Chunze caption’s stated 104 years is likewise retained as the source’s claim, without independent chronological certification.

[^39]: **Joining is not necessarily a canonical stem transformation.** 庚丁相合, Geng and Ding joining, is present in both S and G617. It is not changed to a standard combining pair merely to fit a familiar table. S’s 紅蜺, crimson rainbow, differs from G’s 虹蜺 and remains a possible poetic reading. The golden coffer and shaping Metal by Fire are the source’s images, not additional natal positions.
